# ferrum-state: behaviors

- **What:** every behavior of the in-scope items (`docs/workflow/specification.md` §5), rendered from `raw/behaviors.jsonl` with `tools/render.py`; hazards and stack notes are in `hazards.md` and `stack.md`.
- **cm6:** `@codemirror/state` 6.7.6, mirror commit 651084c; `mirrors/...` line numbers refer to it.
- **Ferrum commit:** ea24c95; line numbers in `docs/` and `tools/` refer to it.
- **Ids:** behaviors `state.<item>.B<n>`; inventory rows `state.cm6.<nnn>`; fixtures `F<nnnn>` in `fixtures/<chunk>.jsonl` of the item's chunk; cm6 tests `<file>#<nn>`

| state.findClusterBreak.B5 | state.cm6.212 | Forward with includeExtending true (the default): after the first code point, every following code point in the package's extending table, generated by mirrors/marijn/find-cluster-break/bin/build-extenders.js from Unicode 17.0.0 GraphemeBreakProperty.txt (:4): every code point below U+30000 (:16-17) whose Grapheme_Cluster_Break is Extend or SpacingMark (:25), minus 31 listed SpacingMark exceptions U+102B, U+102C, U+1038, U+1062-U+1064, U+1067-U+106D, U+1083, U+1087-U+108C, U+108F, U+109A-U+109C, U+1A61, U+1A63, U+1A64, U+AA7B, U+AA7D, U+11720, U+11721 (:9-14, :26-29) is absorbed (isExtendingChar). Absorbed: combining marks, variation selectors U+FE00-U+FE0F, emoji modifiers U+1F3FB-U+1F3FF, the keycap U+20E3, ZWNJ U+200C, Indic viramas such as U+094D, and spacing marks such as U+093F, U+0903 and U+0E33 (UAX #29 GB9a; F0049, F0053, F7001-F7003). The listed exceptions are not joined (U+102B, U+1A61: F7005, F7006). Extend code points at or above U+30000 are outside the table and end the cluster: tag characters U+E0020-U+E007F (a subdivision flag splits after U+1F3F4 and after each tag character, F0022, F7009) and variation selectors U+E0100-U+E01EF (F7007, F7008). F7000 records every code point that joins after "a": 2283 code points in 332 ranges from U+0300 to U+1F3FF with includeExtending true (U+200D among them through the ZWJ rule, B7), only U+200D with false. Code points before the scan start are not consulted. | state.findClusterBreak.B9 | state.cm6.212 | Rules of UAX #29 the package does not implement, with their observable results: CR LF is two clusters (no GB3, F0044); CR, LF and controls do not end a cluster, so a following code point of the extending table or a ZWJ joins them (no GB4/GB5: "\r\u0301x" from 0 is 2, "\n\u200dx" is 3, "\t\u0301x" is 2, "\u0000\u093fx" is 2, F7011-F7014), while a control is not joined to the code point before it ("a\r\u0301" from 0 is 1, F7015); Hangul jamo L, V, T sequences split per jamo and an LV syllable does not join a following T jamo (no GB6-GB8; precomposed syllables are single code units); Prepend such as U+0600 does not join what follows (no GB9b); a consonant, virama, consonant sequence breaks after the virama (no GB9c: "\u0915\u094d\u0937x" gives breaks 2 and 3, F0023, F7016, F7017); Extend code points at or above U+30000 (tag characters, variation selectors U+E0100-U+E01EF) are not joined (GB9 only below U+30000, B5, F7007-F7009); ZWJ joins whatever follows, with no Extended_Pictographic test (GB11 approximated, B7). Implemented through the table: Extend (GB9) and SpacingMark (GB9a) below U+30000, so U+093F joins its base (F0049, F0053). Two pictographs without ZWJ are two clusters, as in UAX #29 (GB999; F0051, F7018). | mirrors/cm6/state/src/char.ts:9 (findClusterBreak) -> mirrors/marijn/find-cluster-break/src/index.js:45-66 (nextClusterBreak: only surrogates, the extending table, ZWJ and regional indicators are handled), table :5-15 | D1, D2 |  | O(1) |  | F0044, F0045, F0046, F0047, F0048, F0049, F0050, F0051, F0052, F0053, F2000, F2001, F2002, F2003, F2004, F2005, F2006, F2007, F2008, F2009, F2010, F2011, F2012, F2013, F2014, F2015, F2016, F2017, F2018, F2019, F2020, F2021, F2022, F2023, F2024, F2025, F2026, F2027, F2028, F2029, F2030, F2031, F2032, F2033, F2034, F2035, F2036, F2037, F2038, F2039, F2040, F2041, F2042, F2043, F2044, F2045, F2046, F2047, F2048, F2049, F2050, F2051, F2052, F2053, F2054, F2055, F2056, F2057, F2058, F2059, F2060, F2061, F2062, F2063, F2064, F2065, F2066, F2067, F2068, F2069, F2070, F2071, F2072, F2073, F2074, F2075, F2076, F2077, F2078, F2079, F2080, F2081, F2082, F2083, F2084, F2085, F2086, F2087, F2088, F2089, F2090, F2091, F2092, F2093, F2094, F2095, F2096, F2097, F2098, F2099, F2100, F2101, F2102, F2103, F2104, F2105, F2106, F2107, F2108, F2109, F2110, F2111, F2112, F2113, F2114, F2115, F2116, F2117, F2118, F2119, F2120, F2121, F2122, F2123, F2124, F2125, F2126, F2127, F2128, F2129, F2130, F2131, F2132, F2133, F2134, F2135, F2136, F2137, F2138, F2139, F2140, F2141, F2142, F2143, F2144, F2145, F2146, F2147, F2148, F2149, F2150, F2151, F2152, F2153, F2154, F2155, F2156, F2157, F2158, F2159, F7007, F7008, F7009, F7011, F7012, F7013, F7014, F7015, F7016, F7017, F7018 |  |

| state.findClusterBreak.B13 | state.cm6.212 | Backward from pos > 1: for candidate c = pos - 2, pos - 3, ... it computes the forward break from c and returns the first result that is lower than the current candidate pos (the loop variable decremented each step), else 0. Observable: from a cluster boundary it returns the start of the previous cluster; from a position inside a cluster (between surrogate halves, between a base and its mark, inside a ZWJ sequence) it returns the start of the cluster containing pos - 1. Walking backward gives the same breaks as walking forward for the strings recorded (F0086-F0089) and for all 163 strings of gen-char.ts (field walksAgree, seed 20261110), with includeExtending true and false. | state.findClusterBreak.B15 | state.cm6.212 | Arguments outside 0..str.length or not integers are not checked (Q9). Forward from pos > length returns pos + 2 (codePointAt gives NaN, codePointSize(NaN) is 2), so findClusterBreak("ab", 5) is 7 and findClusterBreak("", 1) is 3. Forward from a negative pos, the first step adds 2 (the code unit at a negative index is NaN, size 2); while the position is still negative, each code unit read is NaN, which isExtendingChar reports as extending (every comparison with NaN is false, so its binary search ends in `return true`), so with includeExtending true it is absorbed two units at a time until the position reaches 0 or 1, and the scan then goes on from there. Odd negative positions reach 1 without reading index 0 ("ab" from -1, -3, -5, -7 is 1; "\u{1F600}a" from -1 or -3 is 1, inside the surrogate pair); even ones reach 0 and take index 0 as the next code point, so the result is 0 unless index 0 holds a table code point or a ZWJ ("ab" from -2 is 0, "\u0301ab" from -2 is 1) (F0097, F0098, F6097, F7022-F7028, F7035). With includeExtending false NaN is not absorbed and the scan stops after the first step: -1 gives 1, -2 gives 0, every pos <= -3 gives pos + 2, a negative result (F7029-F7031, F7035). The empty string behaves the same (F7032-F7035). Backward from pos > length returns length, for every length (F0099, F0100, F0149, F7036); backward from a negative pos returns 0 (B12, F7036). A fractional pos gives fractional results (1.5 -> 2.5 forward, 0.5 backward) because charCodeAt truncates the index; NaN forward returns NaN, NaN backward returns 0. | mirrors/cm6/state/src/char.ts:9 (findClusterBreak) -> mirrors/marijn/find-cluster-break/src/index.js:17-26 (isExtendingChar with NaN), :45-55, :68-75 (nextClusterBreak, prevClusterBreak) | D1, D2 |  | O(1) |  | F0095, F0096, F0097, F0098, F0099, F0100, F0101, F0102, F0103, F0104, F0105, F0148, F0149, F0150, F6097, F6098, F6099, F7022, F7023, F7024, F7025, F7026, F7027, F7028, F7029, F7030, F7031, F7032, F7033, F7034, F7035, F7036 |  |

| state.TextIterator.next.B1 | state.cm6.225 | Raw forward cursor (iter() or iter(1)), next() without skip: yields each non-empty line's whole content and "\n" for each line break, in document order, then done. Empty lines yield nothing of their own (two consecutive breaks show as two "\n"); the empty document is done at the first call. Reverse reads at q = p - skip: done when q <= 0; "\n" when q - 1 is a break; otherwise the part of the line before q. Skips cross whole tree children without visiting them when skip exceeds the child's length. | state.Text.replace.B4 | state.cm6.234 | Node receiver fast path: when the inserted text has fewer lines than the receiver and [from, to] lies inside one child (from >= child start and to <= child end, so a range ending exactly at a child end counts), the child is replaced recursively; when the updated child's line count stays strictly between totalLines >> 6 and totalLines >> 4 (totalLines being the new document's line count), the result is a node whose children array is a copy with only that child replaced, every other child shared by identity with the receiver (F0950, F0951); otherwise the receiver's replace runs the general path on that child's whole range with the updated child as the inserted text. | mirrors/cm6/state/src/text.ts:281-302 (TextNode.replace) | D1, D2, D6 |  | O(depth * children per node + 32) for a local edit | test-text#05, test-text#10, test-text#12, test-text#27 | F0950, F0951, F1508, F6053, F6060, F6067, F6074, F6081 |  |
| state.Text.replace.B5 | state.cm6.234 | General path (Text.replace): the receiver's part before from, the inserted text and the part after to are decomposed into a flat list of whole children and cut leaves, cut leaves at the two seams joined line-to-line (a joined leaf over 32 lines is split at half), then rebuilt by TextNode.from: fewer than 32 lines in total gives one flat leaf; otherwise chunk = max(32, lines >> 5), children over 2 * chunk lines are opened, children over chunk / 2 lines stand alone, adjacent small leaves merge while their total stays <= 32 lines, and the rest group into nodes of at most chunk lines. Whole children inside the kept ranges, including children of the inserted Text, are reused by identity. Shapes recorded: deleting all but 20 positions of 200 lines gives one leaf (F0952); ten 600-character inserts into a line keep [32 x 6, 8] (F0953); inserting 500 one-character lines gives F0954; 1999 appends give 59 leaves and one nested node (F0955). | mirrors/cm6/state/src/text.ts:54-61 (Text.replace), :185-200 (TextLeaf.decompose), :269-279 (TextNode.decompose), :333-373 (TextNode.from) | D1, D2, D6 |  | O(log n * children per node) for the decomposition plus O(lines inserted / 32) for new leaves; the receiver is never materialized as a string | test-text#04, test-text#05, test-text#06, test-text#07, test-text#09, test-text#12 | F0952, F0953, F0954, F0955, F4300, F5001, F5002, F6054, F6061, F6068, F6075, F6082 |  |

| state.ChangeDesc.B1 | state.cm6.148 | Representation. `sections` is a flat array of pairs (len, ins): len is the span length in the old document; ins is -1 for an unchanged span, otherwise the length of the replacement (insertion (0,n), deletion (n,0), replacement (n,m)). Values built by ChangeSet.of, compose and map go through addSection, which (in this order) drops a section with len 0 and ins <= 0; extends the previous section's len when ins <= 0 equals the previous ins (adjacent unchanged spans merge, adjacent deletions merge: deleting 2-4 and 4-6 of 8 gives the sections (2,-1),(4,0),(2,-1)); adds ins to the previous section when len is 0 and the previous len is 0 (insertions at one point merge); joins into the previous section when forceJoin; otherwise appends. So adjacent replacements stay separate ([[1,'A'],[1,'B'],1]), and a deletion followed by an insertion at its end, or an insertion followed by a deletion at the same point, stay two sections. | mirrors/cm6/state/src/change.ts:21 (ChangeDesc); mirrors/cm6/state/src/change.ts:22-26 (comment); mirrors/cm6/state/src/change.ts:384-391 (addSection) | D1 |  | Memory 2 numbers per section; sections are O(number of changes), independent of document size. | test-change#28 | F0001, F0002, F0003, F0004, F0005, F6000, F6001 |  |

| state.ChangeSpec.B5 | state.cm6.135 | Runtime dispatch is duck-typed: Array.isArray, then `instanceof ChangeSet`, else the value is read as an object without checks. A plain ChangeDesc passed as a spec has no `from`, passes the range check (undefined comparisons are false) and is skipped silently; {insert: "x"} without `from` produces a section of length NaN ([[NaN,"x"]], length NaN); string positions "1"/"2" are compared and subtracted as JS values and give sections of numbers; an `insert` that is neither a string nor a Text (7) is used as a Text and breaks later (toJSON throws TypeError). Inclusive ends grow with insertions at them, exclusive ones do not (RangeValue.endSide.B2). | mirrors/cm6/state/src/rangeset.ts:146-151 | D1 |  | O(changes) per range | test-rangeset#11, test-rangeset#13, test-rangeset#14, test-rangeset#16 | F0038, F0039, F0044, F0045, F0046, F0047, F0048, F0049, F0050, F0068, F0069, F6010, F6011, F6012, F6013, F6014, F6015, F6016, F6017 |  |
| state.RangeSet.map.B6 | state.cm6.184 | Mapped ranges are re-laid within the chunk: a range that still starts at or after the previous kept end (with sides) is appended; an empty one that falls before it is reinserted at the last earlier slot where it fits, scanning backwards while the earlier range does not start after it; anything else is spilled to a new layer. The new chunk starts at the first mapped range's new from (also when that range is spilled) and holds positions relative to it; its maxPoint is recomputed from the kept points; a chunk with no kept range is removed. Examples: points a(1, startSide 1), b(2, startSide -1) deleting [1,2] -> b, a both at 1 in two layers; [0,3] sides -1/1 with a TrackDel point at 5 (side -1) and a point at 6 (side 0) deleting [3,6] -> the point at 5 is dropped, the point at 6 lands at 3 in a second layer; [0,4]/(point 5) deleting [3,5] -> point at 3 spilled. | mirrors/cm6/state/src/rangeset.ts:152-177 (Chunk.map) |  |  | O(r) per range in the worst case (backward scan) | test-rangeset#19, test-rangeset#21, test-rangeset#22, test-rangeset#23, test-rangeset#24 | F0128, F0129, F0130, F0131, F0132, F0133, F0134, F0135, F0141 |  |
| state.RangeSet.map.B7 | state.cm6.184 | A chunk strictly covered by one changed section (touchesRange returns 'cover') is dropped without mapping its ranges, so ranges that would survive Chunk.map (MapMode.Simple points, sides 0/0 or -1/1) disappear when their chunk is covered and survive when a neighbour in the same chunk lies outside the change. After the end (value null, from = to = 1e9, also after further next() calls and after goto past the end) the rank does not depend on the layer reported last: with exactly one layer in all the sets the cursor is that LayerCursor and keeps its set's index; with two or more layers it is the lowest set index among the non-empty sets, because every ended layer cursor sits at from = to = 1e9 with startSide 0 and the heap orders equal positions by rank. With minPointSize 0 a set whose only point sits in a lower layer (top maxPoint -1) is dropped: old {mark [0,10]}, new {mark [0,10]} + point at 5 in layer 2 -> minPointSize -1 reports comparePoint(5, 5, null, p), minPointSize 0 and 1 report nothing; with the point in the top layer minPointSize 0 reports it. | state.RangeSet^compare.B2 | state.cm6.188 | Shared chunks are found among the top layers only: findSharedChunks reads `set.chunk` of each compared set, never `nextLayer`. A top-layer chunk with maxPoint <= 0 (no point, or only empty points, F7014) that appears (same object) in the top layer of a set in both groups, at the position the diff maps the old one to, and that the diff does not touch, is put in the skip set given to every layer cursor of both sides: its ranges are never read (a throwing `value` getter on them is never hit, F0177) and its values are not compared. A chunk holding a non-empty point (maxPoint > 0) is never shared and is walked (F7015). A chunk shared in a nested layer is walked: RangeSet.of([a(0,10), b(2,4)]) compared with itself never reads the top chunk and reads the nested chunk's values twice (F7012). Because the skipped ranges are absent from both sides' active arrays and stretch boundaries, the comparator calls can differ from those for equal content in distinct chunks: with a mark a [0,4] in a top chunk shared by both sides and nested marks b [2,6] (old) and c [2,6] (new), compare reports compareRange(2, 6, [b], [c]); with an equal but distinct top chunk on the new side it reports (2, 4, [a, b], [a, c]) and (4, 6, [b], [c]) (F7013). Equal content in distinct chunks is walked (600 eq calls for 600 equal but distinct values in F0177, whose calls are the same, none). | mirrors/cm6/state/src/rangeset.ts:570-582 (findSharedChunks), :380-381 (compare), :606-612, :645-652 (LayerCursor skip) | D6 |  | O(top-layer chunks) to find shared chunks + walk of every unshared chunk of every layer | test-rangeset#29 | F0177, F7012, F7013, F7014, F7015 |  |

Same name and message as the leaf case (state.Text.lineAt.B5, text.ts:177); the throw site depends on the document's tree shape. Same name and message as the leaf case (state.Text.line.B3, text.ts:177). Fixtures: 40 ASCII lines (F5202); 41 lines with astral characters, CRLF and LF breaks and U+2028 inside a line (F5203). | state.Text.iter.B3 | state.cm6.239 | iter(0) on a node document (more than 32 lines): the constructor sets the root offset to children.length << 1 (dir > 0 is false, text.ts:414); the first next() finds neither the end test (`offset == 0`, text.ts:423) nor the line-break test (`(offsetValue & 1) == 1`, text.ts:432) true, takes the node branch and reads `top.children[offset]`, one past the last child, so `next.length` throws TypeError "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'length')" at text.ts:451. Leaf documents throw the same TypeError from the leaf branch at text.ts:444 (state.Text.iter.B2, F1014). Fixtures: 40 ASCII lines (F5204); 41 lines with astral characters, CRLF and LF breaks and U+2028 inside a line (F5205). Both stack-verified at text.ts:451 by oracle/cm6/text/probe-gapfill.ts. | mirrors/cm6/state/src/text.ts:412-415 (RawTextCursor constructor), :417-457 (RawTextCursor.nextInner; throw at :451), :463-469 (RawTextCursor.next) | D2 |  | O(1) before the throw |  | F5204, F5205 |  |
| state.Text.iter.B4 | state.cm6.239 | dir outside 1 \| -1 on a node document (more than 32 lines): the cursor steps offsets by dir (text.ts:433, :443). With dir 2 it reads every string of the first leaf it descends into, stepping over the break bits as on a leaf (B2); when that leaf ends, the parent's offset is incremented by one (text.ts:429), which lands on a break bit, and the doubled step then reports breaks and passes over children without entering them. Recorded on 70 lines (children [32, 32, 6]): iter(2) yields the non-empty lines of the first child (L0-L30), then "\n" twice with lineBreak true, then done, so the 38 lines of the other children are never yielded; iter(-2) yields the non-empty lines of the last child in reverse, then "\n" twice, then done; iter(3) yields lines and "\n" values from the first and last children only and did not throw (F7037-F7042: "\n", CRLF, lone CR and U+2028-separator sources give the same values; with U+2028 as line content the values carry it, and "\u2028" alone stands for an otherwise empty line). The leaf results differ (B2: iter(2) yields every non-empty line; iter(3) on 4 lines throws). | mirrors/cm6/state/src/text.ts:412-415 (RawTextCursor constructor), :417-461 (RawTextCursor.nextInner: parent offset + 1 at :429, break test :432-439, leaf step :443, node branch :449-458) | D2 |  | linear in the lines of the first (or last) child; the other children are not visited |  | F7037, F7038, F7039, F7040, F7041, F7042 |  |
| state.Text.replace.B7 | state.cm6.234 | Fractional positions on a node document (more than 32 lines; Q9) change lines away from the edit, and differently from a leaf document. Recorded on 40 lines (children [32, 8], k0 = children[0].length) from six sources ("\n", CRLF, lone CR, U+2028 as content, U+2028 as lineSeparator, astral lines; F7045, F7047, F7049, F7051, F7053, F7055): replace(0, 0.5, Text.of(["Q"])) keeps 40 lines but cuts the first child's last line by one unit ("L31" becomes "L3"); when that line is empty (astral source) it is dropped (39 lines). The same call on a 32-line leaf keeps every line (F7057). Mechanism: TextLeaf.replace gives the edited child a cached length half a unit below its content (text.ts:206); the node's fast path rejects the 32-line child by its balance test (text.ts:291-292) and runs the general path over the child's whole range (text.ts:297), where TextLeaf.decompose re-reads the child up to its cached length (appendText(..., 0, text.length), text.ts:190) and String.prototype.slice truncates the last line (text.ts:388). A position inside the implied break between two children (k0 + 0.5) counts as the end of the first child: replace(k0 + 0.5, k0 + 0.5, Q) appends Q to the first child's last line and keeps the break (40 lines, length + 1), while on a leaf a position inside a break (line end + 0.5) joins the two lines around it (F7057: "L30QL31", 31 lines). replace(k0 - 0.5, k0 + 0.5, Q) replaces the last unit of the first child's last line and keeps the break; replace(k0 + 0.5, k0 + 1, Q) joins the two lines and rebuilds the tree ([19, 20]). | mirrors/cm6/state/src/text.ts:54-61 (Text.replace), :185-200 (TextLeaf.decompose), :202-209 (TextLeaf.replace), :269-279 (TextNode.decompose), :281-302 (TextNode.replace), :384-396 (appendText), :580-583 (clip) | D1, D2 |  | as Text.replace B4 and B5 |  | F7045, F7047, F7049, F7051, F7053, F7055, F7057 |  |
